0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}$}}}}}\times\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}\longrightarrow\underline{\mathcal{S}}
.

The category of
symmetric monoidal
\mathscr{T}
–categories
is defined as
\cmonoid(\mathrm{Cat}_{\mathscr{T}})\simeq\func(\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{op}},\cmonoid(\mathrm{Cat}))
. Thus, the datum of a symmetric monoidal
\mathscr{T}
–category is, roughly speaking, given by the datum of a symmetric monoidal category
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(v)
for every
v\in\mathscr{T}
, and for every morphism
f\colon v\rightarrow w
in
\mathscr{T}
, the contravariant functoriality map
f^{*}\colon\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(w)\rightarrow\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(v)
is equipped with a symmetric monoidal structure.

Remark 4.1.2
.

Here, we have only mentioned symmetric monoidal
\mathscr{T}
–categories, i.e., objects in
\cmonoid(\mathrm{Cat}_{\mathscr{T}})
. Using the adjectives of atomic orbitality below, Nardin and Nardin–Shah
[
Nar17
,
NS22
]
have studied the more sophisticated notion of
\mathscr{T}
–symmetric monoidality which also captures the so–called Hill–Hopkins–Ravenel norms in equivariant homotopy theory. This will not play a role in this article.

In order to begin considering more algebraic aspects in the parametrised theory, we will need more conditions on the base category
\mathscr{T}
. For this, we will need the following piece of notation:

Notation 4.1.3
.

Let
\mathscr{T}
be a small category. Let
\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathscr{T}}\subseteq\presheaf(\mathscr{T})=\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T}}
be the smallest subcategory of
\presheaf(\mathscr{T})
containing
\mathscr{T}
and closed under finite coproducts. This has the universal property of the finite–coproduct–cocompletion of
\mathscr{T}
.

Definition 4.1.4
.

Let
\mathscr{T}
be a small category. We say that it is
orbital
if
\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathscr{T}}
admits pullbacks;
atomic
if for all
f\colon v\rightarrow w
and
r\colon w\rightarrow v
such that
rf
is an equivalence, then
f
and
r
were already equivalences;
inductive orbital
(cf.
[
Wil17
, Def. 1.10]
) if it is an orbital
1
–category with finitely many isomorphism classes of objects and morphisms such that every endomorphism in
\mathscr{T}
is an equivalence.

For every
v\in\mathscr{T}
, if
\mathscr{T}
is atomic orbital (resp. inductive orbital), then so is
\mathscr{T}_{/v}
. Also, it is immediate to see that inductive orbitality implies atomic orbitality.

Example 4.1.5
.

The key example of an inductive orbital category for us is that of
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
, the 1–category of finite sets up to size
d
and surjections. We refer the reader to
[
Nar16
, Ex. 4.2]
for more examples.

Recollections 4.1.6

(Parametrised (co)limits)
.

Let
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}},\underline{I}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{\mathscr{T}}
, and
I\colon\underline{I}\rightarrow\underline{\ast}
be the unique functor. If they exist, we call the left adjoint (resp. right adjoint) to the restriction functor
I^{*}\colon\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\rightarrow\underline{\func}(\underline{I},\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})
the
\underline{I}
–shaped parametrised colimit (resp. limit) in
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
.
One special class of parametrised (co)limits are those indexed by the
constant
\mathscr{T}
–category
, i.e., those
\underline{I}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{\mathscr{T}}
of the form a constant
\mathscr{T}
–indexed diagram
\underline{\operatorname{const}}_{\mathscr{T}}(J)
with value
J
for some
J\in\mathrm{Cat}
. Such (co)limits are called
fibrewise (co)limits
. Shah provided in
[
Sha23
, Cor. 5.9]
a criterion for when fibrewise (co)limits exist, namely:
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
admits
\underline{\operatorname{const}}_{\mathscr{T}}(J)
–(co)limits if and only if for each
v\in\mathscr{T}
, the category
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(v)
admits
J
–shaped (co)limits, and for every map
f\colon v\rightarrow w
in
\mathscr{T}
, the functor
f^{*}\colon\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(w)\rightarrow\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(v)
preserves
J
–shaped (co)limits. When we say that a
\mathscr{T}
–category admits initial objects/final objects/coproducts/products/pushouts/pullbacks/filtered colimits/geometric realisations etc., we will mean that the
\mathscr{T}
–category admits the fibrewise (co)limits associated to these diagrams.

Another important special class of parametrised (co)limits are the ones coming from objects in
\mathscr{T}
as follows: let
v\in\mathscr{T}
. We may view it as an object in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\mathscr{T}}
via the inclusion
\mathscr{T}\subseteq\presheaf(\mathscr{T})=\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T}}\subseteq\mathrm{Cat}_{\mathscr{T}}
, which we denote as
\underline{v}
. Writing
v\colon\underline{v}\rightarrow\underline{\ast}
for the unique map, we may speak of
v
–(co)limits. Such (co)limits are called indexed (co)products. It was in fact shown in
[
Sha23
, §12]
that all parametrised colimits may be built from fibrewise colimits and finite indexed coproducts. When
\mathscr{T}
is orbital, a
\mathscr{T}
–category admits all indexed coproducts if and only if (1) for all morphisms
f\colon v\rightarrow w
in
\mathscr{T}
, the functor
f^{*}\colon\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(w)\rightarrow\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(v)
admits a left adjoint
f_{!}
and (2) for all pullbacks in
\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathscr{T}}
as in the left square in

(14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{i}Z_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i}\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(Z_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(v)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(w)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sqcup_{i}\overline{f}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sqcup_{i}\overline{g}_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i}\overline{g}_{i!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Rightarrow}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i}\overline{f}_{i}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}

the adjointed square of categories given on the right commutes. This gives a “double coset decomposition” in the setting of orbital base categories. Importantly, when
\mathscr{T}
is also atomic, the double coset decomposition for
f^{*}f_{!}
always contains a copy of
\mathrm{id}_{V}
which allows one to define a semiadditivity norm map, cf.
4.1.8
. By passing to the opposite category, analogous statements also hold for parametrised limits.

Furthermore, using these fibrewise criteria for the existence of fibrewise (co)limits and indexed (co)products, we may also characterise when a functor
F\colon\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\rightarrow\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}
preserves parametrised (co)limits, namely for example,
F\colon\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(v)\rightarrow\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}(v)
preserves colimits in the ordinary sense, and for every
f\colon v\rightarrow w
, the adjointed square
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(v)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}(v)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(w)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}(w)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Leftarrow}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}

coming from the commutation datum of
F
with
f^{*}
commutes.

Recollections 4.1.7

(Parametrised presentability)
.

Write
\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}_{\mathscr{T}}\coloneqq\func(\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{op}},\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}})
for the category of large
\mathscr{T}
–categories. Within this, we may find the nonfull subcategory
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}}
of
presentable
\mathscr{T}
–categories
and left adjoint functors. The theory of parametrised presentable categories is much the same as in the ordinary world, for instance, there is also the adjoint functor theorem in this context. Moreover, presentable
\mathscr{T}
–categories may be characterised as a
\mathscr{T}
–category which admits all parametrised colimits and which are fibrewise presentable (cf.
[
Hil24a
, Thm. 6.1.2 (7)]
). There is a symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}}
refining the Lurie tensor product, whose tensor unit is the
\mathscr{T}
–category
\underline{\mathcal{S}}
of
\mathscr{T}
–spaces. Objects in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}})\subset\cmonoid(\mathrm{Cat}_{\mathscr{T}})
are called
presentably symmetric monoidal
\mathscr{T}
–categories
, and they may be viewed as the symmetric monoidal
\mathscr{T}
–categories whose underlying
\mathscr{T}
–category is presentable and whose tensor product commutes with parametrised colimits in each variable.

Aside from making transparent in what way the category
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
organises many of the structures in Goodwillie calculus, one of the key reasons for us to take the parametrised point of view is that, while spectral Mackey functors do not admit a nice universal property in categories akin to the one enjoyed by the category of spectra, it
does so
when viewed as a parametrised category. The following set of recollections will explain this.

Recollections 4.1.8

(Parametrised semiadditivity and stability)
.

Suppose
\mathscr{T}
is atomic orbital. Let
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
be a pointed
\mathscr{T}
–category (i.e., the fibrewise initial and final objects coincide). Using the hypothesis of atomic orbitality, Nardin
[
Nar16
]
constructed for every morphism
f\colon v\rightarrow w
in
\mathscr{T}
a canonical comparison transformation
f_{!}\longrightarrow f_{*}

of functors
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(v)\rightarrow\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(w)
. We then say that the pointed
\mathscr{T}
–category
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
is
\mathscr{T}
–semiadditive
if it is fibrewise semiadditive and all these comparison transformations are equivalences. Additionally, we say that
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
is
\mathscr{T}
–stable
if it is
\mathscr{T}
–semiadditive and fibrewise stable. The universal example of a
\mathscr{T}
–stable category is given by
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
, the
\mathscr{T}
–category of
\mathscr{T}
–spectra
which at
v\in\mathscr{T}
is given by spectral
\mathscr{T}_{/v}
–Mackey functors
\mathchoice{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.2.
Universal spaces and stratifications

In this subsection, we introduce the universal space associated to “families”, extending the notion from equivairant homotopy theory to all atomic orbital categories
\mathscr{T}
. These will be the gadgets we use to organise our stratification arguments later. Using these universal spaces, we record a categorification of geometric fixed points following
[
HKK24
]
. Throughout, we will assume that
\mathscr{T}
is an atomic orbital category, unless otherwise specified.

Construction 4.2.1
.

Let
f\colon\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\subseteq\mathscr{T}
be a sieve, i.e., if there is a map
y\rightarrow x
where
x\in\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}
and
y\in\mathscr{T}
, then
y\in\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}
as well. We then define
E\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T}}
as
f_{!}\ast
where
\ast\in\func(\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{S})
is the final object. We claim now that

E\mathcal{F}(v)\simeq\begin{cases}\emptyset&\text{ if }v\notin\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\\
\ast&\text{ if }v\in\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}.\end{cases}

To see this, since
f
is fully faithful, the value of
E\mathcal{F}
at
v\in\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}
is clear; if
v\notin\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}
, then by the pointwise Kan extension formula, we just need to compute
\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{(\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathrm{op}})_{/v}}f
. But since
v\notin\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}
and
\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}
is a sieve, we get that
(\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathrm{op}})_{/v}=\emptyset
, and so the colimit is
\emptyset
as well, as wanted.

By adding a basepoint to the unique map
E\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\ast
, we obtain a map
E\mathcal{F}_{+}\rightarrow S^{0}
in
\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T},*}
. We then define
\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T},*}
as the cofibre

(15)

E\mathcal{F}_{+}\longrightarrow S^{0}\longrightarrow\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}.

Since evaluations preserve (co)limits, we obtain the expected description

\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}(v)\coloneqq\begin{cases}S^{0}&\text{ if }v\notin\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\\
\ast&\text{ if }v\in\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}.\end{cases}

Remark 4.2.2
.

The object
\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T},*}
is easily seen to be an idempotent algebra under the pointwise smash product symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T},*}
. Hence, it defines a nonunital symmetric monoidal idempotent endofunctor
\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\wedge-\colon\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T},*}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T},*}
. Writing
s\colon\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{T}
for the inclusion of the cosieve complement to
\mathscr{T}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
, it is easy to see that
\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\wedge-
yields the symmetric monoidal Bousfield localisation
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T},*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\func(\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{S}_{*})\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}}(\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T},*}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{*}}

In particular,
\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}
enjoys the following universal property: for
A,X\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T},*}
with
X(v)\simeq\ast
for all
v\in\mathscr{T}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
, the map
A\rightarrow\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\wedge A
induces an equivalence

\map_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T},*}}(\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\wedge A,X)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\map_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T},*}}(A,X).

Notation 4.2.3
.

Suppose that
\mathscr{T}
is an inductive orbital category with a final object
t\in\mathscr{T}
. We write
\mathscr{T}_{{\mathcal{P}}}\subseteq\mathscr{T}
for the full subcategory away from the final object
t\in\mathscr{T}
. This is a sieve because if
x\rightarrow y
is a map in
\mathscr{T}
where
y\neq t
, then
x\neq t
also because all endomorphisms are equivalences by inductive orbitality. This thus yields a universal space
\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}
.

In equivariant homotopy theory, the cofibre sequence
Equation

15
underlies one of the most important inductive techniques in the subject, namely that of “isotropy separation”. Such methods have been considered by Glasman
[
Gla17
]
and Wilson
[
Wil17
]
in the more general parametrised setting. We will now record some points from this philosophy as a basis for our stratification arguments later, following closely and slightly expanding the “categorified” treatment given in
[
HKK24
, §2.2]
. Unless otherwise specified,
\mathscr{T}
will be an inductive orbital category for the rest of the subsection.

In the first two constructions, we will set the stage by declaring the various categories and functors for presentable categories that we need.

Construction 4.2.4
.

\mathscr{T}
be an atomic orbital category. Then by

, Prop. 2.2.19]

with a symmetric monoidal structure making the Bousfield localisation

to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}_{\mathscr{T}}\otimes-\colon\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}-\mathrm{st}}
into a smashing localisation.

Construction 4.2.5
.

Let
\mathscr{T}
be an inductive orbital category,
\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\subseteq\mathscr{T}
a sieve, and write
\iota\colon\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}\subseteq\mathscr{T}
be the inclusion of the complement of the sieve. By
[
Wil17
, Lem. 1.14]
,
\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}
is yet again an inductive orbital category. In particular, it makes sense to speak of
\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}
–stability. The inclusion
\iota
induces by the right Kan extension the inclusion
\iota_{*}\colon\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}_{\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}}\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}_{\mathscr{T}}
which is a symmetric monoidal functor with respect to the cartesian symmetric monoidal structures. This identifies the source of
\iota_{*}
as the full subcategory of the target consisting of those
\mathscr{T}
–categories
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
with the property that for every
v\in\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}
,
v^{*}\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\simeq\underline{\ast}\in\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}_{\mathscr{T}_{/v}}
.

Note that the inclusion
\iota_{*}
induces the fully faithful functor
\iota_{*}\colon\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}-\mathrm{st}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}-\mathrm{st}}
because parametrised stability (cf.
4.1.8
) is the requirement of being fibrewise stable and for every morphism
f\colon u\rightarrow v
in the base category, the canonical transformation of functors
f_{!}\Rightarrow f_{*}
of functors
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(u)\rightarrow\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(v)
is an equivalence.

Next, we give the standard formulas for the (co)tensors over pointed spaces.

Construction 4.2.6
.

For
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}\mathrm{-st}}
and
X\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T},*}
(and writing
\underline{X}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{\mathscr{T},*}
for the corresponding pointed
\mathscr{T}
–category), we define

X\wedge\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\coloneqq\cofib(\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\simeq\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{\ast}\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\rightarrow\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{X}\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})

\underline{\func}_{*}(\underline{X},\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})\coloneqq\fib_{0}(\underline{\func}(\underline{X},\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})\rightarrow\underline{\func}(\ast,\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})\simeq\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}).

By
[
HKK24
, Cons. 2.2.23]
,
X\wedge\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
satisfies the universal property of being the tensoring of
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{{\mathscr{T}}-\mathrm{st}}
over
\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T},*}
. Importantly, via the equivalence
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}}\simeq(\mathrm{Pr}^{R}_{\mathscr{T}})^{\mathrm{op}}
from for example
[
Hil24
, Prop. 2.2.15]
, we may compute
X\wedge\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
as
\underline{\func}_{*}(\underline{X},\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})
, using that
\lim_{X}\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\simeq\underline{\func}(\underline{X},\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})
since
\underline{\mathrm{Pr}}^{R}\subseteq\underline{\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}}
is closed under arbitrary parametrised limits by
[
Hil24
, Lem. 2.5.5]
. In particular,
Y_{+}\wedge\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\simeq\underline{\func}(\underline{Y},\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})
for any
Y\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T}}
.

Remark 4.2.7
.

The map
\underline{\func}_{*}(\underline{X},\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})\rightarrow\underline{\func}(\underline{X},\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})
is fully faithful because it is pulled back from the fully faithful inclusion
0\hookrightarrow\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
. As such, pointed functors from groupoids to pointed categories may really be viewed as a property of a functor.

With the preliminaries out of the way, we may now obtain the following important categorification of geometric fixed points for arbitrary inductive orbital base categories
\mathscr{T}
.

Proposition 4.2.8
.

Let
\mathscr{T}
be an inductive orbital category and
\iota\colon\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\subseteq\mathscr{T}
a sieve. Then the inclusion
\iota_{*}\colon\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}-\mathrm{st}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}-\mathrm{st}}
from
4.2.5
participates in a smashing localisation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}-\mathrm{st}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}-\mathrm{st}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\iota}^{*}=\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\wedge-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{*}}

using the symmetric monoidal structures from
4.2.4
.

Proof.

By
[
HKK24
, Lem. 2.1.32]
and the same arguments as in
[
HKK24
, Thm. 2.2.26]
, we have the smashing localisation
\widetilde{\iota}^{*}=\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\wedge-\colon\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}}\rightleftharpoons\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0mu\iota_{*}
which simultaneously identifies
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}}
as both the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}}\cap\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}_{\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}}
and as
\mathrm{Mod}_{\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\wedge\underline{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathscr{T}}}(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}})
. On the other hand, for any
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}-\mathrm{st}}
, we also have that
\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\wedge\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\simeq\underline{\func}_{*}(\underline{\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}},\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})
(cf.
4.2.6
), and so it is in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}-\mathrm{st}}\cap\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}_{\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}}=\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}-\mathrm{st}}
since parametrised stability is closed under cotensoring. Thus, the top Bousfield localisation restricts to the bottom one in

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}-\mathrm{st}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}-\mathrm{st}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\iota}^{*}=\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\wedge-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchoice{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip In particular, this makes the solid squares commute and so the bottom map
\widetilde{\iota}^{*}
inherits a lax symmetric monoidal structure.

4.2.4

∎

The tensoring construction allows us to obtain a stable recollement dictated by
\mathcal{F}
. Since we will need some standard facts about parametrised stable recollements in a form which is not recorded in the literature, we first justify with the following lemma why we may quote the results from
[
CDH+25
]
.

Lemma 4.2.9
.

The evaluation functor
\prod_{v\in\mathscr{T}}\mathrm{ev}_{v}\colon\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{{\mathscr{T}}-\mathrm{st}}\rightarrow\prod_{v\in\mathscr{T}}\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}
preserves and reflects fibrewise (co)limits.

Proof.

The proof proceeds exactly as in
[
Hil24
]
where only the case of
\mathscr{T}
–perfect–stable categories was written, and so we only briefly indicate the steps. One first argues that
\mathrm{Pr}^{R}_{\mathscr{T}-\mathrm{st}}\subset\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}_{\mathscr{T}}
is closed under fibrewise limits exactly as in
[
Hil24
, Lem. 2.5.5]
, but now without having to check
\kappa
–accessibility of functors. Next, one shows that
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{{\mathscr{T}}-\mathrm{st}}\subset\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}_{\mathscr{T}}
preserves fibrewise limits as in
[
Hil24
, Prop. 2.5.6]
: since one already knows that
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}\subset\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}
preserves limits, the key points now which are handled
mutatis mutandis
as in
loc. cit.
is to show that
\lim_{i\in I}\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}_{i}
is
\mathscr{T}
–stable and that for any map
\operatorname{const}_{I}\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}\rightarrow\{\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
in
\func(I,\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}-\mathrm{st}})
, the induced map
\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}\rightarrow\lim_{i\in I}\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}_{i}
preserves
\mathscr{T}
–coproducts. All in all, as in
[
Hil24
, Lem. 2.5.10]
, we get that for all
v\in\mathscr{T}
,
\mathrm{ev}_{v}\colon\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{{\mathscr{T}}-\mathrm{st}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}
preserves (co)limits by using that
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}},\mathrm{Pr}^{R}_{\mathrm{st}}\subset\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}
are closed under limits and the identification
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{{\mathscr{T}}-\mathrm{st}}\simeq(\mathrm{Pr}^{R}_{\mathscr{T}-\mathrm{st}})^{\mathrm{op}}
from
[
Hil24
, Prop. 2.2.15]
. Given these ingredients, we may now immediately conclude as in
[
Hil24
, Thm. 2.5.11]
, using conservativity of
\prod_{v\in\mathscr{T}}\mathrm{ev}_{v}
.
∎

Proposition 4.2.10
.

Let
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{{\mathscr{T}}-\mathrm{st}}
. Then we have a stable recollement
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\func}_{*}(\underline{\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}},\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\func}(\underline{E\mathcal{F}},\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{*}}

where the top sequence is identified with
{E\mathcal{F}}_{+}\wedge\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\rightarrow S^{0}\wedge\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\rightarrow\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\wedge\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
. Furthermore, the endofunctor
p^{*}p_{!}
is given by tensoring with
\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{T},*}
. We thus also write
p_{!}
as
\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\otimes-
.

Proof.

By
4.2.6
, we already know that
{E\mathcal{F}}_{+}\wedge\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\rightarrow S^{0}\wedge\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\rightarrow\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\wedge\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
is a cofibre sequence in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{{\mathscr{T}}-\mathrm{st}}
by the universal property of tensorings. On the other hand, it was also explained that these may be identified with the functor categories upon passing to
\mathrm{Pr}^{R}_{\mathscr{T}-\mathrm{st}}
, which gives the middle sequence in the statement where
j^{*}
also admits a fully faithful right adjoint
j_{*}
; passing to left adjoints gives the top cofibre sequence in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{{\mathscr{T}}-\mathrm{st}}
where
j_{!}
is also fully faithful. Thus, by
[
CDH+25
, Cor. A.1.10 (ii)]
, we know that the top sequence is a fibre sequence as well. Thus, now by
[
CDH+25
, Lem. A.2.8]
, we also get the bottom two fibre and cofibre sequence. Note that we are able to quote these results from
[
CDH+25
]
because fully faithfulness and (co)fibre sequences in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}-\mathrm{st}}
are checked fibrewise by virtue of
4.2.9
. Finally, the statement describing
p^{*}p_{!}
is because we have a cofibre sequence
j_{!}j^{*}\rightarrow\mathrm{id}_{\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}}\rightarrow p^{*}p_{!}
, and
E\mathcal{F}_{+}\otimes-\colon\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\rightarrow\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
is by definition given by
j_{!}j^{*}
.
∎

To end this subsection, we show that the abstract category
\underline{\func}(\underline{E\mathcal{F}},\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})
admits a concrete description as a subcategory of
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
.

Proposition 4.2.11
.

Let
\mathscr{T}
be inductive orbital,
\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\subseteq\mathscr{T}
be a sieve, and
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}-\mathrm{st}}
. Let
\langle\mathcal{F}\rangle\subseteq\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
be the smallest
\mathscr{T}
–stable–presentable full subcategory containing all of
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}(v)
for all
v\in\mathcal{F}
.

Then
\im(j_{!}\colon\underline{\func}(\underline{E\mathcal{F}},\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})\rightarrow\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})=\langle\mathcal{F}\rangle
. In particular, the map
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\rightarrow\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\wedge\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
may be identified as the Verdier quotient
\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\rightarrow\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}/\langle\mathcal{F}\rangle
which may be computed fibrewise.

. Therefore,
\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\wedge\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
computes the cofibre in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathscr{T}-\mathrm{st}}
of the inclusion
\langle\mathcal{F}\rangle\hookrightarrow\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}
. Since we know that, in the nonparametrised setting, the fibres of the cofibres of such inclusions is the original localising subcategory itself, we deduce from
4.2.9
that
\underline{\func}(\underline{E\mathcal{F}},\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})\simeq\fib(\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\rightarrow\widetilde{E\mathcal{F}}\wedge\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})\simeq\langle\mathcal{F}\rangle
, as required.
∎

We illustrate this principle with an example that we will use later in
Section

5.3
.

Example 4.2.12
.

By
[
Gla17
,
Wil17
]
, we know that the inclusion
\iota\colon\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}}\subseteq\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathscr{T}}
admits a right adjoint
p
(note that this right adjoint does not exist before taking the finite coproduct cocompletion) which sends any coproduct factor in
\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}=\mathscr{T}\backslash\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}
to
\emptyset
. On the other hand, we have the inclusion
j\colon\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\subseteq\mathscr{T}
. Then by
[
Gla17
, Thm. 2.32]
and
[
Wil17
, Nota. 1.33]
, we have the stable recollement

(16)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mackey(\mathscr{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}};\mathrm{Sp})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mackey(\mathscr{T};\mathrm{Sp})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mackey(\mathscr{T}_{{\mathcal{F}}};\mathrm{Sp}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{*}}

Here, the essential image of the inclusion
p^{*}
is given precisely by those
\mathscr{T}
–Mackey functors which vanish on
\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\subseteq\mathscr{T}
.

Now, recall that
\mackey(\mathscr{T};\mathrm{Sp})
is a compactly rigidly generated category with the set of self–dual compact generators given by
\{\Sigma^{\infty}\text{\char 71\relax}(v)\>|\>v\in\mathscr{T}\}
where
\Sigma^{\infty}\text{\char 71\relax}\colon\spanCat(\mathscr{T})\rightarrow\func^{\times}(\spanCat(\mathscr{T}),\mathrm{Sp})=\mackey(\mathscr{T};\mathrm{Sp})
given by the Yoneda map. By the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\spanCat(\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\func^{\times}(\spanCat(\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}),\mathrm{Sp})=\mackey(\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}};\mathrm{Sp})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\spanCat(\mathscr{T})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\func^{\times}(\spanCat(\mathscr{T}),\mathrm{Sp})=\mackey(\mathscr{T};\mathrm{Sp})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{\infty}\text{\char 71\relax}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{\infty}\text{\char 71\relax}}

coming from the naturality of the Yoneda embedding along left Kan extensions, we thus see that the inclusion
j_{!}\colon\mackey(\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}};\mathrm{Sp})\hookrightarrow\mackey(\mathscr{T};\mathrm{Sp})
identifies with the inclusion of the localising subcategory generated by
\{\Sigma^{\infty}\text{\char 71\relax}(v)\>|\>v\in\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\}
, which is also the set coming from the images of the tensor unit under the induction
v_{!}\colon\mackey(\mathscr{T}_{/v};\mathrm{Sp})\rightarrow\mackey(\mathscr{T};\mathrm{Sp})
for
v\in\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}}
.

Therefore, all in all, if
\mathscr{T}
has a final object
t
, then
\mackey(\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{F}};\mathrm{Sp})=\langle\mathcal{F}\rangle(t)
for
\langle\mathcal{F}\rangle\subseteq\mathchoice{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip at

.

4.3.
A recognition principle for spectra

Let
\mathscr{T}
be an inductive orbital category. We give a list of checkable axioms that guarantees that a
\mathscr{T}
–category is symmetric monoidally equivalent to
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
, essentially axiomatising the arguments in
[
MNN17
]
and
[
CMN+24
, App. A]
. Throughout this subsection, let
\underline{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{{\mathscr{T}}-\mathrm{st}})
be a
\mathscr{T}
–stable presentably symmetric monoidal category.

Notation 4.3.1
.

Since
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}
\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}

is fibrewise given by the Day convolution on Mackey functors.

Recall from
4.2.3
that when
\mathscr{T}
has a final object, we have the universal space
\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}
by virtue of inductive orbitality of
\mathscr{T}
. Furthermore, recall also from
4.2.8
that we have a Bousfield localisation
\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}\otimes-\colon\underline{\mathcal{A}}\rightarrow\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}\wedge\underline{\mathcal{A}}
associated to the endofunctor
\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}\otimes-
on
\underline{\mathcal{A}}
. In fact, this upgrades even to a smashing localisation since
\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}\in\underline{\mathcal{S}}_{*}
is an idempotent algebra. By
4.2.11
, this Bousfield localisation may also be identified with the Verdier quotient of
\underline{\mathcal{A}}
by everything coming from
\underline{\mathcal{A}}(v)
for
v\neq t
.

We write
\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}\in\underline{\mathcal{A}}
for the object
\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\underline{\mathcal{A}}}\in\underline{\mathcal{A}}
. Moreover, we will also write
\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}_{v}
for the analogous universal space for
\mathscr{T}_{/v}
. Since
\mathscr{T}_{/v}
has a final object,
\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}_{v}
will always make sense even if
\mathscr{T}
does not have a final object.

Hypothesis 4.3.2
.

Suppose
\underline{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies that:

(1)

for every
v\in\mathscr{T}
, the representables
\{f_{!}\mathbbm{1}_{\underline{\mathcal{A}}(u)}\}_{(f\colon u\rightarrow v)\in\mathscr{T}_{/v}}
in
\underline{\mathcal{A}}(v)\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}})
form a set of compact generators. Note that
\mathscr{T}
–stability already implies that the representables are self–dual, and in particular, dualisable;

(2)

for every
v\in\mathscr{T}
, we have an equivalence
\mapsp_{\underline{\mathcal{A}}(v)}(\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}_{v},\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}_{v})\simeq\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{Sp}
.

0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{A}}
from
4.3.1
is an equivalence.

Since equivalences are checked by basechanging to
\mathscr{T}_{/v}
for all
v\in\mathscr{T}
, we will assume for the rest of the subsection that
\mathscr{T}
has a final object
t\in\mathscr{T}
.

Observation 4.3.4
.

Writing
\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}
for
\underline{\mathcal{A}}(t)
where
t\in\mathscr{T}
is the final object, we obtain a unique symmetric monoidal colimit–preserving functor
\mathrm{infl}\colon\mathrm{Sp}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}
by virtue of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}
being a presentably symmetric monoidal stable category. The lax symmetric monoidal right adjoint is then formally given by
\mapsp_{\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}}(\mathbbm{1},-)
.

We write
\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}[\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}]\in\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}
for the category
(\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}\wedge\underline{\mathcal{A}})(t)
.

Lemma 4.3.5
.

Suppose
\underline{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies
4.3.2
. Then the composite
\mathrm{Sp}\xlongrightarrow{\mathrm{infl}}\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}\xlongrightarrow{\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}\otimes-}\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}[\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}]
is an equivalence with inverse given by the right adjoint
\mapsp_{\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}}(\mathbbm{1},\mathrm{incl}-)
.

Proof.

As in
[
MNN17
, Thm. 6.11]
, we first note that
\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}[\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}]
is generated by the compact unit
\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}
because
\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}[\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}]
is the Verdier quotient by the proper representables and
\underline{\mathcal{A}}
was generated by representables by
4.3.2
(1). By the symmetric monoidal version of Schwede–Shipley, we just need to show that the endomorphism
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
–ring spectrum of
\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}
is given by
\mathbb{S}
. Since there is a unique
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
–ring structure on
\mathbb{S}
, it suffices to show that the endomorphism spectrum is equivalent to
\mathbb{S}
, and this is precisely supplied by
4.3.2
(2).
∎

Definition 4.3.6

(Geometric fixed points)
.

We define the
geometric fixed points functor

\Phi^{t}
to be the following symmetric monoidal colimit preserving functor

\Phi^{t}\colon\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}\xlongrightarrow{\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}\otimes-}\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}[\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}]\subseteq\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}\xlongrightarrow{\mapsp(\mathbbm{1},-)}\mathrm{Sp}

More generally, for any
u\in\mathscr{T}
, we may define similarly the functor
\Phi^{u}\colon\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
as the symmetric monoidal colimit preserving composition
\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}\xlongrightarrow{u^{*}}\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}_{/u}}\xlongrightarrow{\Phi^{u}}\mathrm{Sp}
.

For the next point, recall that
E\mathcal{P}\in\func(\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{S})
is equivalent to the colimit
\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{v\in\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{P}}}v_{!}\ast
where
v_{!}\colon\func(\mathscr{T}_{/v}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\func(\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{S})
. Indeed, observe that
\ast\simeq\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{P}}}\text{\char 71\relax}\in\func(\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{S})
where
G
is the Yoneda embedding. Then, since
E\mathcal{P}\simeq f_{!}\ast
by
4.2.1
where
f_{!}\colon\func(\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{S})\hookrightarrow\func(\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{S})
is the left Kan extension, the equivalence follows.

Proposition 4.3.7
.

Let
M\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}
. Then
M\simeq 0
if and only if
\Phi^{u}M\simeq 0\in\mathrm{Sp}
for all
u\in\mathscr{T}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathscr{T}
is inductive orbital,
\mathscr{T}
has finitely many objects and morphisms, so we will prove the statement by induction. By induction, suppose that
v^{*}M\simeq 0
for all
v\in\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{P}}
. Then
E\mathcal{P}_{+}\otimes M\simeq\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{u\in\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{P}}}(v_{!}v^{*}M)\simeq\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{v\in\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{P}}}0\simeq 0
.
Now the assumption together with
4.3.5
gives us that
\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}\otimes M\simeq 0
since
\Phi^{t}M\simeq 0
. Hence, by the cofibre sequence

E\mathcal{P}_{+}\otimes M\rightarrow M\rightarrow\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}\otimes M
,
we get
M\simeq 0
as required.
∎

4.3.3
.

\mathscr{T}

0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{A}}
.

By
[
BDS16
, Thm. 1.3]
, because both categories are generated under colimits by dualisable objects by
4.3.2
(1), this right adjoint
R
itself admits a right adjoint, and
\mathrm{U}_{\mathcal{A}}\dashv R
satisfies the projection formula. Furthermore,
R
is conservative because its left adjoint
\mathrm{U}_{\mathcal{A}}
sends generators to generators, again by
4.3.2
(1). Thus, by the monadicity result of
[
MNN17
, Prop. 5.29]
, we know that
\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathscr{T}}}(R(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}}))
. Hence we have reduced our problem merely to showing that the adjunction unit

\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathscr{T}}}\longrightarrow R\mathrm{U}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathscr{T}}})\simeq R(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}})

is an equivalence.

By induction, this map becomes an equivalence upon applying
\Phi^{u}
for all
u\in\mathscr{T}_{\mathcal{P}}
. Hence, by
4.3.7
, it suffices to check that it is an equivalence upon applying
\Phi^{t}
where
t\in\mathscr{T}
is the final object. Since
\Phi^{t}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathscr{T}}})\simeq\mathbb{S}
by symmetric monoidality, and we are looking at a map of commutative algebras, it suffices to see that there is an equivalence of spectra
\Phi^{t}R(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}})\simeq\mathbb{S}
. This follows from the following computation:

\begin{split}\Phi^{t}R(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}})&=\mapsp_{\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathscr{T}}}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathscr{T}}},\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}\otimes R\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}})\\
&\simeq\mapsp_{\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathscr{T}}}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathscr{T}}},R(\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}}))\\
&\simeq\mapsp_{\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}},\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}})\\
&\simeq\mapsp_{\mathcal{A}_{\mathscr{T}}}(\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}},\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}})\simeq\mathbb{S}\end{split}

where the second equivalence is by the
\mathrm{U}_{\mathcal{A}}\dashv R
projection formula and the last equivalence is by
4.3.2
(2).
∎

5.
Parametrised perspective on Goodwillie calculus

5.1.
Subdiagonal functors as a parametrised category

The goal of this subsection is to prove
B
that the subdiagonal functors assemble into a presentably symmetric monoidal
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
–stable category. This enhances the ordinary category
\mathrm{Exc}_{d}=\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,1}}
with the requisite parametrised structure, see
5.1.3
. The restriction maps will be the cross–effects and the (co)inductions will be induced by the diagonal maps, as in
2.2.8
.

As a first step, we assemble the subdiagonal functors for arbitrary
{\mathcal{C}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}
to an
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
–category.

Construction 5.1.1
.

Fix stable categories
{\mathcal{C}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}
and suppose that
{\mathcal{C}}
is small. We first construct an object in
\func_{*}\in\func(\mathrm{Epi}_{d},\mathrm{Cat})
given schematically by
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\func_{*}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\func_{*}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\func_{*}^{d-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\func_{*}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[2]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[d-1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[d]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{*}}

where
\func_{*}^{r}
denotes the category
\func_{*}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})
. To this end, consider the composition
\underline{\delta}_{{\mathcal{C}}}\colon\mathrm{Epi}_{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\subset\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{op}}\xlongrightarrow{\func(-,{\mathcal{C}})}\mathrm{Cat}
. This implements the data of diagonals on
{\mathcal{C}}
represented schematically as
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}}^{\times 2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}}^{\times(d-1)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}}^{\times d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[2]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[d-1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[d]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}

The object
\func_{*}\in\func(\mathrm{Epi}_{d},\mathrm{Cat})
is then defined to be the composite

\func_{*}\colon\mathrm{Epi}_{d}\xlongrightarrow{\underline{\delta}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{op}}\xlongrightarrow{\func_{*}(-,{\mathcal{D}})}\mathrm{Cat}.

Next,
2.2.6
(2) guarantees that we may restrict to the subcategories
\mathrm{Exc}_{d,r}^{\lrcorner}\subseteq\func_{*}^{r}
to get an object
\widetilde{\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}}_{d}^{\lrcorner}\in\func(\mathrm{Epi}_{d},\mathrm{Cat})
given schematically by
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Exc}_{{d,2}}^{\lrcorner}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Exc}_{d,d-1}^{\lrcorner}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[2]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[d-1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[d]}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{*}}

Finally, passing to the cross–effect (bi)adjoints from
2.2.8
, we get the desired
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
–category of
d
–excisive functors

\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}_{d}^{\lrcorner}\in\func(\mathrm{Epi}_{d}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Cat})
.

Our next goal is to prove that this object is
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
–presentable stable.

Lemma 5.1.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a small stable category and let
{\mathcal{D}}
be a cocomplete stable category. Then
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
admits arbitrary
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
–indexed coproducts.

Proof.

By
4.1.6
, it suffices to show that the (bi)adjoints of

\mathrm{cr}_{f}\colon\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{d,r}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})\to\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{d,k}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}),\;\;f\colon[k]\twoheadrightarrow[r]\in\mathrm{Epi}_{d}

from
2.2.8
enjoy the double-coset formula. But this is precisely supplied by
2.3.7
.
∎

With this in place, we will prove the desired presentable stability statement.

Theorem 5.1.3
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a small stable category and let
{\mathcal{D}}
be a presentable stable category. Then the
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
–category
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
is a presentable
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
–stable category.

Proof.

By
3.1.1
, we know that the
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
–category is fibrewise presentable and stable. By
5.1.2
,
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
admits parametrised colimits. Therefore, by
[
Hil24a
, Thm. 6.1.2]
, we get that it is
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
–presentable. In fact, from
2.2.8
(1), since the “induction” functors
(\Delta\times\mathrm{id})^{*}
are biadjoint, we even get that
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}
is
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
–semiadditive. Thus, by definition (cf.
4.1.8
), it is an
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
–stable category.
∎

In the presence of multiplicative structures on
{\mathcal{C}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}
, we may endow
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
with a symmetric monoidal structure, i.e., it may be enhanced to an object in
\cmonoid(\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}_{\mathrm{Epi}_{d}})
which is furthermore fibrewise presentably symmetric monoidal.

Construction 5.1.4
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{ex}})
and
{\mathcal{D}}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}})
. We first construct
\underline{\func}_{\underline{\ast}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times\bullet},{\mathcal{D}})^{\otimes}\in\cmonoid(\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}_{\mathrm{Epi}_{d}})
. First, recall Lurie’s functorial Dwyer–Kan localisation from
[
Lur17
, Cons. 4.1.7.1]
in the left diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{WCat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cmonoid(\mathrm{WCat})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cmonoid(\mathrm{Cat})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{equiv}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{equiv}}

where
\mathrm{equiv}
assigns to a category
{\mathcal{C}}
the pair
({\mathcal{C}},W)
where
W\subset{\mathcal{C}}
is the wide subcategory consisting of equivalences. Importantly, the functor
L\colon\mathrm{WCat}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
is product–preserving. Hence, it induces the adjunction on the right. There is also a finite–product–preserving functor
\mathrm{all}\colon\mathrm{Cat}\rightarrow\mathrm{WCat}
sending a category
{\mathcal{C}}
to the pair
({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{C}})
. Given these, now consider the composite

\begin{split}(\mathrm{Day}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times\bullet},{\mathcal{D}})^{\otimes},\mathrm{all})\colon\mathrm{Epi}_{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{op}}&\xlongrightarrow{({\mathcal{C}}^{\times\bullet})^{\otimes}}\cmonoid(\mathrm{Cat})\\
&\xlongrightarrow{\mathrm{Day}(-,{\mathcal{D}})^{\otimes}}\cmonoid(\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}})\xhookrightarrow{\mathrm{all}}\cmonoid(\mathrm{W}\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}})\end{split}

where
\mathrm{Day}(-,{\mathcal{D}})^{\otimes}
is the Day convolution symmetric monoidal structure on the functor category
\func(-,{\mathcal{D}})
covariantly functorial along left Kan extensions.
Next, recall from
2.1.7
that the Bousfield localisation
(-)^{\mathrm{red}}\colon\func({\mathcal{C}}^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})\rightarrow\func_{\underline{\ast}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})
canonically refines to a symmetric monoidal Bousfield localisation. In particular, if we write
W_{r}
for the collection of morphisms inverted by this Bousfield localisation (so that
\func_{\underline{\ast}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})\simeq\func({\mathcal{C}}^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})[W_{r}^{-1}]
), we know that it is a
\otimes
–ideal. Moreover, for any surjection
f\colon[k]\twoheadrightarrow[r]
, since the solid square in
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\func({\mathcal{C}}^{\times k},{\mathcal{D}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\func({\mathcal{C}}^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\func_{\underline{\ast}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times k},{\mathcal{D}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\func_{\underline{\ast}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{*}_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\mathrm{red}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{f!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\mathrm{red}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{*}_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{f!}}

commutes, so does the dashed square of left adjoints. Thus, we see that the functor
\Delta_{f!}
from
\func({\mathcal{C}}^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})
to
\func({\mathcal{C}}^{\times k},{\mathcal{D}})
sends
W_{r}
to
W_{k}
. Observe also that by virtue of the dashed commuting square, the functor
\Delta_{f!}\colon\func_{\underline{\ast}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})\rightarrow\func_{\underline{\ast}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times k},{\mathcal{D}})
is the cross–effect functor
\mathrm{cr}_{f}
by construction.

All in all, since the
W_{r}
’s are tensor ideals and are preserved by
\Delta_{f!}
, we may pointwise pass to the subfunctor

(\mathrm{Day}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times\bullet},{\mathcal{D}})^{\otimes},W_{\bullet})\subset(\mathrm{Day}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times\bullet},{\mathcal{D}})^{\otimes},\mathrm{all})\in\func(\mathrm{Epi}_{d}^{\mathrm{op}},\cmonoid(\mathrm{W}\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}})).

Postcomposing with
L\colon\func(\mathrm{Epi}_{d}^{\mathrm{op}},\cmonoid(\mathrm{W}\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}))\rightarrow\func(\mathrm{Epi}_{d}^{\mathrm{op}},\cmonoid(\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}))
yields the desired enhancement of
\func_{\underline{\ast}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times\bullet},{\mathcal{D}})
together with the cross–effect functors to an object in
\func(\mathrm{Epi}_{d}^{\mathrm{op}},\cmonoid(\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}))
.

Now, write
\mathrm{Opd}
for the category of operads, so that we have a nonfull inclusion
\cmonoid(\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}})\subset\mathrm{Opd}
. This says that it is a property for an operad to be a symmetric monoidal category and it is a property for a map of operads between symmetric monoidal categories, i.e., lax symmetric monoidal functors, to be a symmetric monoidal functor. So far, we have an object
\underline{\func}_{\underline{\ast}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times\bullet},{\mathcal{D}})\in\func(\mathrm{Epi}_{d}^{\mathrm{op}},\cmonoid(\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}))
using the cross–effects as the transition maps. By
3.1.4
, we obtain the suboperad of
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
–categories
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})\subseteq\underline{\func}_{\underline{\ast}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times\bullet},{\mathcal{D}})\in\func(\mathrm{Epi}_{d}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Opd})
. By the same proposition, this suboperad has the property that it is fibrewise a symmetric monoidal category, and that the transition maps of operads are in fact symmetric monoidal functors. Hence, we get that
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
is in fact an object in
\func(\mathrm{Epi}_{d}^{\mathrm{op}},\cmonoid(\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}))\subset\func(\mathrm{Epi}_{d}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Opd})
, as required. By construction,
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
is fibrewise presentably symmetric monoidal.

With these preparations, the main result of this subsection is now rather easy.

Theorem 5.1.5
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{ex}})
and
{\mathcal{D}}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}})
. The
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
–stable category
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
admits an
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
–presentably symmetric monoidal structure which is fibrewise given by the Day convolution.

Proof.

We already know that it is fibrewise presentably symmetric monoidal by
5.1.4
. It thus suffices now to show that tensoring preserves indexed coproducts. In other words, for every surjection
f\colon[k]\twoheadrightarrow[r]
,
G\in\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
, and
F\in\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,k}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
, the canonical projection formula map
\Delta_{f}^{*}(F\otimes\mathrm{cr}_{f}G)\longrightarrow(\Delta_{f}^{*}F)\otimes G
in
\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}
is an equivalence. But this is precisely
3.1.7
.
∎

5.2.
The main theorem and a summarising dictionary

We now collect all the ingredients and prove the main result of the article. We first need to connect the notion of geometric fixed points functor as defined in
4.3.6
to that of the localisations studied in
Section

3.2
, which we do in
5.2.3
.

Notation 5.2.1
.

For
r\leq d
, we write
\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r}\coloneqq(\mathrm{Epi}_{d})_{/[r]}
. In general, for
r\leq b\leq d
, we have inclusions
\iota_{d,b,r}\colon\mathrm{Epi}_{b,r}\subseteq\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r}
and
\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{b,r}}\subseteq\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}
. By
4.2.8
, we get a smashing localisation associated to
\iota_{d,b,r}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{d,b,r}(-)\coloneqq\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}_{d,b,r}\otimes(-)\colon\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r}\mathrm{-st}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Epi}_{b,r}\mathrm{-st}}.}

where
\mathcal{P}_{d,b,r}
is the family in
\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r}
of those objects
[k]\twoheadrightarrow[r]
such that
k>b
.

In the case of
b=r
, it will be convenient to adopt the notation
\mathcal{P}_{d,r}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}_{d,r,r}
and
\Phi^{d,r}\coloneqq\Phi^{d,r,r}
. That is,
\mathcal{P}_{d,r}
is the family of objects in
\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r}
away from the final object
([r]=[r])
.

Notation 5.2.2
.

For
r\leq d
, we write
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r}})
for the image of
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}
under the restriction functor
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Epi}_{d}})\rightarrow\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r}})
induced by the forgetful map
\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r}\rightarrow\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
.

Lemma 5.2.3
.

Let
r\leq b\leq d
. Evaluating the adjunction unit
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}\rightarrow\Phi^{d,b,r}\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}
at
(f\colon[k]\twoheadrightarrow[r])\in\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r}
yields the map
P^{\lrcorner}_{b,k}\colon\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,k}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{b,k}}
. In particular, evaluating
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}\rightarrow\Phi^{{d,r}}\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}
at the final object
([r]=[r])\in\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r}
yields the functor
P_{\underline{1}}\colon\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Exc}_{\underline{1}}\simeq\mathrm{Sp}
.

Proof.

As in
4.2.11
, we write
\langle\mathcal{P}_{d,b,r}\rangle\subseteq\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}
for the smallest presentable stable subcategory containing
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}(x)
for all
x\in\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r}\backslash\mathrm{Epi}_{b,r}
. Then it is easily seen that
\langle\mathcal{P}_{d,b,r}\rangle([r]=[r])
is the localising subcategory generated by the set as in
3.2.5
. Therefore, by
3.2.5
and
4.2.11
, we obtain the desired conclusion.
∎

We are at last ready to state and prove the main
C
. For this, recall from
5.1.5
and
4.3.1
that we have a unique symmetric monoidal left adjoint functor
\mathrm{U}\colon\mathchoice{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip .

.

be a positive integer. The symmetric monoidal functor

to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}_{d}\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}
is an equivalence of objects in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Epi}_{d}\mathrm{-st}})
.

Proof.

By
3.2.4
, we get that
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}
satisfies
4.3.2
(1), and by
5.2.3
,
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}
satisfies
4.3.2
(2). Therefore, by
4.3.3
, we obtain the desired conclusion.
∎

For example, a straightforward consequence of
5.2.4
is the following:

Corollary 5.2.5
.

Let
d\geq 1
,
{\mathcal{C}}
be a small stable category, and
{\mathcal{D}}
a presentable stable category. Then
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
admits a unique module structure in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Epi}_{d}}
over
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}
. Moreover, if
{\mathcal{C}}
is exact symmetric monoidal and
{\mathcal{D}}
is presentably symmetric monoidal, then
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
attains a unique
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}
–commutative algebra structure in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Epi}_{d}}
.

Proof.

By
5.1.3
,
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
is
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
–presentable–stable, and therefore, by
[
Hil24
, Prop. 2.2.19]
, it admits a unique module structure over the idempotent algebra
\mathchoice{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}_{d}
, which by
5.2.4
, is equivalent to
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}
. The statement about symmetric monoidal structures is now also clear.
∎

To end this subsection, we offer a picture that contextualises our work by comparing it to the equivariant situation. To this end, let us first recall the situation in the latter setting.

Let
G
be a finite group,
N\triangleleft G
be a normal subgroup, and
H,K\leq G
be subgroups such that
N\leq H
, and let
\overline{H}\leq G/N
denote the image of
H
under
G\twoheadrightarrow G/N
. There are then two orthogonal and complementary perspectives on how to present the datum of a genuine
G
–spectrum, namely via its
genuine fixed points
and
geometric fixed points
, respectively. The categories governing these data may be summarised by the following diagram of indexing categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{G/N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\overline{H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
genuine fixed points
geometric fixed points

where we have used the standard identification
\mathcal{O}_{H}\simeq(\mathcal{O}_{G})_{/G/H}
to obtain the upward–pointing arrows. In this way, we see that the horizontal direction governs geometric fixed point functorialities, and the vertical direction governs the restriction/induction/genuine fixed points functorialities.

Coming back to the setting of calculus, the analogue is then the following:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Epi}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Epi}_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Epi}_{4}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Epi}_{5}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Epi}_{2,2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Epi}_{3,2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Epi}_{4,2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Epi}_{5,2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Epi}_{3,3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Epi}_{4,3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Epi}_{5,3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Epi}_{4,4}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Epi}_{5,4}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Epi}_{5,5}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
genuine
geometric

where now the vertical direction involves the cross–effects and restrictions along the diagonal as in
2.2.6
, and the horizontal direction involves the excisive approximations from
5.2.3
(and hence also the derivatives since these may be computed by a combination of excisive approximations and cross–effects). It is worth emphasising here that unlike in the equivariant situation, we do
not
have an equivalence
\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r}\coloneqq(\mathrm{Epi}_{d})_{/[r]}\simeq\mathrm{Epi}_{r}
, and this accounts for some fundamental qualitative differences between the two settings.

Combining
5.2.3
with
5.2.4
and
Example

4.2.12
, the stable recollement associated to the inclusion
\mathrm{Epi}_{b,r}\subseteq\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r}
for
r\leq b\leq d
looks like

(17)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{b,r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mackey(\mathrm{Epi}_{d\backslash b,r};\mathrm{Sp}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{\lrcorner}_{b,r}}

where
P^{\lrcorner}_{b,r}
is the “geometric fixed points” functor. This specialises to the usual stable recollement

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Exc}_{d-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Exc}_{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}^{B\Sigma_{d}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{d-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{d-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((-)\otimes(\bullet)^{\otimes d})_{h\Sigma_{d}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((-)\otimes(\bullet)^{\otimes d})^{h\Sigma_{d}}}

associated to Kuhn’s squares
[
Kuh04
]
by setting
b=d-1
,
r=1
and
P^{d-1}
is McCarthy’s dual calculus approximation
[
McC01
]
.

We now summarise the foregoing discussion in a dictionary translating between genuine equivariant stable homotopy theory and stable Goodwillie calculus, extending the partial one of

5.3.
Application: Mackey model for all multiexcisive functors

Because the equivalence in
5.2.4
is that of parametrised categories, it is therefore compatible with the natural stratifications on both sides. In this short subsection, we show how to exploit this observation to deduce a Mackey model for all multiexcisive functors without any cutoff degrees of excisiveness. Of some note will be the disappearance of the subdiagonality conditions when one passes to infinity.

Notation 5.3.1
.

We write
\mathrm{Epi}_{\infty,r}
for
\mathrm{Epi}_{/[r]}
, so that
\mathrm{Epi}_{\infty,1}=\mathrm{Epi}
. Let
{\mathcal{D}}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}
. We write
\mackey_{\mathrm{fs}}(\mathrm{Epi}_{\infty,r};{\mathcal{D}})\subseteq\mackey(\mathrm{Epi}_{\infty,r};{\mathcal{D}})
for the full subcategory of finitely supported Mackey functors. By applying
{\mathcal{D}}\otimes-
to the stable recollement from
Example

4.2.12
, we get inclusions
p^{*}\colon\mackey(\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r};{\mathcal{D}})\hookrightarrow\mackey(\mathrm{Epi}_{d+1,r};{\mathcal{D}})
with essential image those
\mathrm{Epi}_{d+1,r}
–Mackey functors that vanish on any
([d+1]\twoheadrightarrow[r])\in\mathrm{Epi}_{d+1,r}
. Taking the sequential colimit along the inclusions
\mackey(\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r};{\mathcal{D}})\xhookrightarrow{p^{*}}\mackey(\mathrm{Epi}_{d+1,r};{\mathcal{D}})\subseteq\mackey(\mathrm{Epi}_{\infty,r};{\mathcal{D}})
, we obtain an identification
\mackey_{\mathrm{fs}}(\mathrm{Epi}_{\infty,r};{\mathcal{D}})\simeq\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{d}\mackey(\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r};{\mathcal{D}})
.

We also write
\mathrm{MultiExc}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})\subseteq\func_{\underline{\ast}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})
for the full subcategory identified as the sequential colimit of
\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{d}\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
where the inclusions
\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})\subseteq\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d+1,r}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
are the colimit preserving right adjoints of
P^{\lrcorner}_{d,r}
. Because the subdiagonality condition involves larger and larger
d
, we see that
\mathrm{MultiExc}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})
is just the full subcategory of functors which are reduced and finite excisive in each of the
r
variables.

Corollary 5.3.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}
. There is an equivalence

\mathrm{MultiExc}((\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega})^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})\simeq\mackey_{\mathrm{fs}}(\mathrm{Epi}_{\infty,r};{\mathcal{D}}).

Proof.

5.2.4
and
5.2.3
, we see that the fibrewise colimit preserving inclusions

0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}_{d,r}
and
\Phi^{d,b,r}\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}\subseteq\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}
are compatible. Thus we get
\mathrm{MultiExc}((\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega})^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})\simeq\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{d}\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,r}}\otimes{\mathcal{D}}\simeq\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{d}\mackey(\mathrm{Epi}_{d,r};{\mathcal{D}})\simeq\mackey_{\mathrm{fs}}(\mathrm{Epi}_{\infty,r};{\mathcal{D}})
as required.
∎

Remark 5.3.3
.

Alternatively, one could try to obtain the identifications for
r\geq 2
from above by applying the identification for
r=1
iteratively via the equivalence of
\mathrm{MultiExc}((\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega})^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})
to the category
\mathrm{MultiExc}((\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega})^{\times(r-1)},\mathrm{Exc}(\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega},{\mathcal{D}}))
. This will then yield the equivalence

\mathrm{MultiExc}((\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega})^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})\simeq\func^{\mathrm{multi}-\times}_{\mathrm{fs}}(\spanCat(\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{Epi}})^{\times r},{\mathcal{D}})

where the right hand side are finitely supported functors which preserve finite products in each of the
r
variables. One might hope that this immediately implies
5.3.2
since
\mathrm{Epi}_{\infty,r}\simeq\mathrm{Epi}^{\times r}
. However, this is not as direct as one might first surmise because it is not true that
\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{Epi}_{\infty,r}}
is equivalent to
(\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{Epi}})^{\times r}
.

5.4.
Application: a special case of the Segal conjecture in calculus

One of the virtues of the symmetric monoidal equivalence
\mackey(\mathrm{Epi}_{d};{\mathrm{Sp}})\simeq\mathrm{Exc}_{d}
is that we can transport the natural
t
–structure on the left to one that can be fruitfully used to filter object on the right in a way which interacts in known ways with the symmetric monoidal structures on both sides. Furthermore, the full parametrised equivalence
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}\simeq\mathchoice{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip
