0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}_{d}
from our main theorem also guarantees that the natural stratifications on boths sides coincide. The purpose of this final subsection is to take advantage of these points to begin an exploration of a Segal conjecture type statement in Goodwillie calculus.

Our first order of business is to formulate the statement of a “Segal conjecture in calculus”, following the example of
[
GM97
]
. This rests on the following two constructions:

Construction 5.4.1

(Goodwillie–Burnside augmentation ideal)
.

As already used in the main body of the article, there is a symmetric monoidal functor
\mathrm{cr}_{d}\colon\mathrm{Exc}_{d}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
admitting a right adjoint
\Delta^{*}
with a preferred lax symmetric monoidal enhancement. This is analogous to the adjunction
\res^{G}_{e}\colon\mathrm{Sp}_{G}\rightleftharpoons\mathrm{Sp}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0mu\mathrm{Coind}^{G}_{e}
from equivariant homotopy theory. We therefore obtain a map in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Sp})

\mapsp_{\mathrm{Exc}_{d}}(\mathbbm{1}_{d},\mathbbm{1}_{d})\longrightarrow\mapsp_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})\simeq\mathbb{S}

taking
\pi_{0}
of which induces a ring map
A(d)\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
where
A(d)
is the Goodwillie-Burnside ring of
[
ABH+25
]
. We call the kernel of this map
I(d)
the
Goodwillie-Burnside augmentation ideal
.

Since
A(d)
is Noetherian,
I(d)
is finitely generated, and so let us write
I(d)=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m})
. Following
[
GM97
]
, we may define the
I(d)
-adic completion of an object in
\mathrm{Exc}_{d}
as follows: let
K(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m})\coloneqq\fib(\mathbbm{1}_{d}\rightarrow\mathbbm{1}_{d}[a_{1}^{-1},\ldots,a_{m}^{-1}])
; for
F\in\mathrm{Exc}_{d}
, we then define its
I(d)
-adic completion as

F^{\wedge}_{I(d)}\coloneqq\mathrm{Hom}(K(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}),F)

where
\mathrm{Hom}
is the internal hom in
\mathrm{Exc}_{d}
.

Construction 5.4.2

(Borelifications)
.

For
F\in\mathrm{Exc}_{d}
, we now construct a map

(18)

F(-)^{\wedge}_{I(d)}\longrightarrow(\partial_{d}F\otimes(-)^{\otimes d})^{h\Sigma_{d}}

in
\mathrm{Exc}_{d}
. As a notation suggestive of the equivariant analogue, we will write the
d
–th derivative functor
\partial_{d}\colon\mathrm{Exc}_{d}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}^{B\Sigma_{d}}
also as
\beta^{*}
, so that
\mathrm{fgt}\circ\beta^{*}\simeq\mathrm{cr}_{d}
where
\mathrm{fgt}\colon\mathrm{Sp}^{B\Sigma_{d}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
is the forgetful functor. This admits a fully faithful right adjoint
\beta_{*}
which sends
X\in\mathrm{Sp}^{B\Sigma_{d}}
to the functor
(X\otimes(-)^{\otimes d})^{h\Sigma_{d}}
. The adjunction unit thus gives us a map
F(-)\longrightarrow(\partial_{d}F\otimes(-)^{\otimes d})^{h\Sigma_{d}}
.

On the other hand, for any
X\in\mathrm{Sp}^{B\Sigma_{d}}
,
\beta_{*}X\in\mathrm{Exc}_{d}
is already
I(d)
–complete in the sense that the map
\beta_{*}X\rightarrow(\beta_{*}X)^{\wedge}_{I(d)}
is an equivalence. To wit, first note that
\beta^{*}(\mathbbm{1}_{d}[a_{1}^{-1},\ldots,a_{m}^{-1}])\simeq 0
since
\mathrm{fgt}\colon\mathrm{Sp}^{B\Sigma_{d}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
is conservative and
\mathrm{cr}_{d}(\mathbbm{1}_{d}[a_{1}^{-1},\ldots,a_{m}^{-1}])\simeq\mathbb{S}[0^{-1}]\simeq 0
, by definition of
I(d)
. Hence,
\beta^{*}K(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m})\rightarrow\beta^{*}\mathbbm{1}_{d}
is an equivalence. Now, by symmetric monoidality of
\beta^{*}
, we see that
(\beta_{*}X)^{\wedge}_{I(d)}\simeq\beta_{*}\mathrm{Hom}(\beta^{*}K(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}),X)\simeq\beta_{*}X
, as claimed.

Therefore, the map
F(-)\rightarrow(\partial_{d}F\otimes(-)^{\otimes d})^{h\Sigma_{d}}
factors through the
I(d)
–adic completion as in
Equation

18
. This completes the construction.

We are now ready to state the theorem.

Theorem 5.4.3
.

Let
d=p
be a prime number. Then the transformation

P_{d}\Sigma^{\infty}\Omega^{\infty}(-)^{\wedge}_{I(d),p}\longrightarrow\big(((-)^{\otimes d})^{h\Sigma_{d}}\big)^{\wedge}_{p}

of
d
–excisive functors
\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
is an equivalence.

We will prove this by reducing it to the usual Segal conjecture in equivariant homotopy theory for the group
C_{p}
via a stratification argument and exploiting the idiosyncrasies of the augmentation ideal
I(d)
. The key new input here is an analysis of the interaction between the stratification functors and the
I(d)
–adic completion. This is captured by
5.4.8
, which we work towards now.

We will exploit the fact that
P_{1}\colon\mathrm{Exc}_{d}\rightarrow\mathrm{Exc}_{1}
is a smashing localisation with a nice description of the idempotent algebra, namely:

Construction 5.4.4
.

Goodwillie gives us a functorial formula for
P_{1}
, namely
P_{1}F\simeq\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{n}\Omega^{n}F\Sigma^{n}
. Now,
\Sigma^{*}\colon\mathrm{Exc}_{d}\rightarrow\mathrm{Exc}_{d}
is an automorphism which in fact is a part of an automorphism of the presentable
\mathrm{Epi}_{d}
–category
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}\simeq\mathchoice{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox 0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}_{d}
, we thus see that there exists an invertible object
T\in\mathrm{Exc}_{d}
such that
\Sigma^{*}(-)\simeq T\otimes-\colon\mathrm{Exc}_{d}\rightarrow\mathrm{Exc}_{d}
. By inputting
F
to be the tensor unit
\mathbbm{1}_{d}
in Goodwillie’s formula, we thus see that
T\simeq\Sigma^{*}\mathbbm{1}_{d}
and we obtain a map
\mathbbm{1}_{d}\rightarrow\Omega T
such that the sequential diagram in the formula is given by
F\simeq\mathbbm{1}_{d}\otimes F\rightarrow\Omega T\otimes F\rightarrow(\Omega T)^{\otimes 2}\otimes F\rightarrow(\Omega T)^{\otimes 3}\otimes F\rightarrow\cdots
. Importantly, we have also that
T(-)\simeq P_{d}\Sigma^{\infty}\Omega^{\infty}(\Sigma-)
and so
T^{m}\coloneqq T^{\otimes m}\simeq P_{d}\Sigma^{\infty}\Omega^{\infty}(\Sigma^{m}-)
. All in all, writing
E_{n}\coloneqq(\Omega T)^{\otimes n}
and
E\coloneqq\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{n}E_{n}
, we learn that
P_{1}F\simeq E\otimes F
.

The strategy is to carry out a connectivity argument to show that
P_{1}(-)\simeq E\otimes-
commutes with nice inverse limits. The key input from calculus will enter by way of
5.4.6
afforded by our full parametrised identification
\mathchoice{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.8pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{-0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{-0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{-0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.8pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.73909pt\raisebox{0.30612pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.56567pt\raisebox{0.56567pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.30612pt\raisebox{0.73909pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}_{d}\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}
, which, in particular, guarantees that the natural stratifications on both sides agree. To this end, let us first record the following known fact about
t
–structures on spectral Mackey functors.

Lemma 5.4.5
.

Let
\mathscr{T}
be an inductive orbital category. Then an object
X\in\mackey(\mathscr{T};\mathrm{Sp})
is connective if and only if
\Phi^{u}X\in\mathrm{Sp}
is connective for all
u\in\mathscr{T}
.

Proof.

For the forward implication, recall that the connective part
\tau_{\geq 0}\mackey(\mathscr{T};\mathrm{Sp})
is generated under colimits by the orbits. But these orbits are suspension spectra, and so since the geometric fixed points commute with suspension spectra and preserve all colimits, we thus see that they send
\tau_{\geq 0}\mackey(\mathscr{T};\mathrm{Sp})
to the subcategory of
\mathrm{Sp}
generated under colimits by the sphere, i.e., connective spectra. For the converse implication, since connectivity of
X
is a pointwise statement, we just need to check that
X(u)\in\mathrm{Sp}
is connective for all
u\in\mathscr{T}
. Thus, by induction along proper families as in
4.2.3
, it suffices to assume that
\mathscr{T}
has a final object
t
and that we already know that
X(u)
is connective for all
u\neq t\in\mathscr{T}
. To see that
X(t)
is connective, recall that we have the cofibre sequence in
\mathrm{Sp}

(E\mathcal{P}_{+}\otimes X)(t)\longrightarrow X(t)\longrightarrow(\widetilde{E\mathcal{P}}\otimes X)(t)=\Phi^{t}X.

By assumption, the right hand term is connective. By the paragraph before
4.3.7
, the left hand term is a colimit whose terms are
X(u)
for
u\neq t
, and so is itself connective. Then so is
X(t)
, as required.
∎

Proposition 5.4.6
.

Let
d\geq 1
. Then the cofibre of
\Omega^{m}T^{m}\rightarrow\Omega^{m+1}T^{m+1}
is
m
-connective.

Proof.

Writing
C
for the cofibre, it suffices by
5.4.5
to show that
\Phi^{[k]}C
is
m
–connective for all
1\leq k\leq d
. We then get the following equivalences in spectra:

\begin{split}\Phi^{[k]}C&\simeq\cofib(\Omega^{m}\Sigma^{m*}\Phi^{[k]}\mathbbm{1}_{d}\rightarrow\Omega^{(m+1)}\Sigma^{(m+1)*}\Phi^{[k]}\mathbbm{1}_{d})\\
&\simeq\cofib\big(\Omega^{m}(\Sigma^{m}\mathbb{S})^{\otimes k}\rightarrow\Omega^{m+1}(\Sigma^{m+1}\mathbb{S})^{\otimes k}\big)\end{split}

where the first equivalence is since
\Sigma^{m*}
is a parametrised automorphism of
\underline{\mathrm{Exc}}^{\lrcorner}_{{d}}
and so commutes with
\Phi^{[k]}
, and the second equivalence is since
\Phi^{[k]}\mathbbm{1}_{d}\simeq\mathrm{cr}_{k}P_{k}\Sigma^{\infty}\Omega^{\infty}(-)\simeq(-)^{\otimes k}
by
5.2.3
. We thus see that
\Phi^{[k]}C
is a cofibre of
(k-1)m
–connective terms when
k>1
and is a cofibre of an equivalence when
k=1
. Thus, it is itself
m
–connective, as was to be shown.
∎

Using this, we roughly follow
[
Gre88
, Lem. 4.4]
from the equivariant setting in showing that geometric fixed points “commute” with completions at the augmentation ideal. For this, we start with the following key classical connectivity argument (cf.
[
Ada74
, Thm. 15.2]
for a variant).

Lemma 5.4.7
.

Suppose we have an inverse system
\{Y_{n}\}_{n}\in\mathrm{Exc}_{d}
of uniformly bounded below objects. Then the map
P_{1}\lim_{n}Y_{n}\rightarrow\lim_{n}P_{1}Y_{n}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

As is standard, since inverse limits may be written as the fibre of a map between infinite products in stable categories, it suffices to deal with the case of infinite products. Using the notations from
5.4.4
, let
{C}_{k}\coloneqq\cofib({E}_{k}\rightarrow{E})
. We claim that
C_{k}
is
k
–connective. Given this, we consider the cofibre sequence

E_{k}\otimes\prod_{n}Y_{n}\simeq\prod_{n}E_{k}\otimes Y_{n}\longrightarrow\prod_{n}E\otimes Y_{n}\longrightarrow\prod_{n}C_{k}\otimes Y_{n}

where we have used that the
E_{k}
’s are invertible for the first equivalence. Since the
Y_{n}
’s were uniformly bounded below and the connectivities of the
C_{k}
’s were increasing strictly, we see that the connectivity of
\prod_{n}C_{k}\otimes Y_{n}
increases strictly as
k
increases. This is because the tensor product of a
k
–connective and
m
–connective object under the Day convolution in
\mackey(\mathrm{Epi}_{d};\mathrm{Sp})
is
(m+k)
–connective, and the infinite product
\prod_{n}
and the
t
–structure on
\mackey(\mathrm{Epi}_{d};\mathrm{Sp})
is given pointwise. Hence,
\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{k}\prod_{n}C_{k}\otimes Y_{n}\simeq 0
. This thus yields that
E\otimes\prod_{n}Y_{n}\simeq\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{k}E_{k}\otimes\prod_{n}Y_{n}\rightarrow\prod_{n}E\otimes Y\simeq\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{k}\prod_{n}E\otimes Y
is an equivalence, as desired.

Now, to prove the claim, recall from the preceding construction that
E_{m}\simeq\Omega^{m}(P_{d}\Sigma^{\infty}\Omega^{\infty}(\Sigma-))^{\otimes m}
. By
5.4.6
, the maps
E_{m}\rightarrow E_{m+1}
are
m
-connective, and therefore the map
E_{m}\rightarrow E
is
m
–connective, as claimed.
∎

Proposition 5.4.8
.

Let
I\subseteq A(d)
be a finitely generated ideal and
F\in\mathrm{Exc}_{d}
be bounded below (i.e.,
(\mathrm{cr}_{k}F)(\mathbb{S},\ldots,\mathbb{S})
is bounded below for all
k
). Then
P_{1}(F^{\wedge}_{I})\simeq(P_{1}X)^{\wedge}_{P_{1}I}\in\mathrm{Sp}
.

Proof.

Since the
I
–completion may be computed by taking successive completion with respect to the generators of
I
, it suffices to deal with the case when
I
is a principal ideal. So let
\alpha\in A(d)
and
F\in\mathrm{Exc}_{d}
be bounded below. Then we need to show that
P_{1}(F^{\wedge}_{\alpha})\simeq(P_{1}F)^{\wedge}_{P_{1}\alpha}\in\mathrm{Sp}
. To see this, recall that
F^{\wedge}_{\alpha}\coloneqq\lim_{n}F/\alpha^{n}
is an inverse limit of uniformly bounded below objects. Hence, we get equivalences

P_{1}(F^{\wedge}_{\alpha})\simeq P_{1}\lim_{n}F/\alpha^{n}\simeq\lim_{n}P_{1}(F/\alpha^{n})\simeq\lim_{n}P_{1}F/(P_{1}\alpha)^{n}\simeq(P_{1}F)^{\wedge}_{P_{1}\alpha}

where the second equivalence is by
5.4.7
and the third equivalence is since
P_{1}\colon\mathrm{Exc}_{d}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
is exact and symmetric monoidal. This completes the proof.
∎

Next, we provide the two computational inputs needed in the proof of
5.4.3
. To state the first of these, recall the Tate construction
(-)^{t\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{nt}}(\Sigma_{p})}
with respect to the family of nontransitive subgroups from
[
ABH+25
, Ex. 10.4, Defs. 10.16 and 10.21]
.

Lemma 5.4.9
.

There is a canonical equivalence
{\mathbb{S}^{t\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{nt}}(\Sigma_{p})}}^{\wedge}_{p}\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\mathbb{S}^{tC_{p}}}^{\wedge}_{p}
of commutative algebras of spectra.

Proof.

Consider the unit morphism

\eta\colon\mathbb{S}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Coind}_{C_{p}}^{\Sigma_{p}}(\mathbb{S})\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Sp}_{\Sigma_{p}}).

By
[
ABH+25
, Lem. 10.11]
,
\eta
induces the morphism

\eta^{t\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{nt}}(\Sigma_{p})}\colon{\mathbb{S}^{t\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{nt}}(\Sigma_{p})}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{S}^{tC_{p}}}.

of commutative algebras, see also
[
ABH+25
, Lem. 10.18]
. We will show that
\eta^{t\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{nt}}(\Sigma_{p})}
is an equivalence after
p
-completion.

Indeed, by the Segal conjecture, the unit morphism
\mathbb{S}\longrightarrow\mathbb{S}^{tC_{p}}
is an equivalence after
p
-completion. Therefore, the homomorphism

\pi_{*}\Big({\eta^{t\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{nt}}(\Sigma_{p})}}^{\wedge}_{p}\Big)\colon\pi_{*}\Big({\mathbb{S}^{t\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{nt}}(\Sigma_{p})}}^{\wedge}_{p}\Big)\longrightarrow\pi_{*}\Big({\mathbb{S}^{tC_{p}}}^{\wedge}_{p}\Big)

is surjective. Finally,
\pi_{*}\Big({\eta^{t\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{nt}}(\Sigma_{p})}}^{\wedge}_{p}\Big)
is also injective by the transfer agrument from
[
GL20
, Prop. A.8]
.
∎

For the next lemma, let us first establish some useful notations.

Notation 5.4.10
.

The symmetric monoidal functors
\mathrm{cr}_{k}\colon\mathrm{Exc}_{d}\rightarrow\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,k}}
and
P_{\underline{1}}\colon\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,k}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
induce maps in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Sp})

\mapsp_{\mathrm{Exc}_{d}}(\mathbbm{1}_{d},\mathbbm{1}_{d})\xlongrightarrow{\mathrm{cr}_{k}}\mapsp_{\mathrm{Exc}^{\lrcorner}_{{d,k}}}(\mathbbm{1}_{d,k},\mathbbm{1}_{d,k})\xlongrightarrow{P_{\underline{1}}}\mapsp_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{S}).

Upon applying
\pi_{0}
, we get a map of commutative rings which we denote as

\Phi^{[k]}\colon A(d)\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}

following the notation for geometric fixed points in equivariant homotopy theory. When
k=d
, the map
\Phi^{[d]}
is nothing but the map constructed in
5.4.1
which is used to define the augmentation ideal.

Lemma 5.4.11
.

Let
d=p
be a prime number. Then

\Big(p,\>\mathrm{Im}\big(\Phi^{[k]}\colon I(d)\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}\big)\Big)=\begin{cases}p\mathbb{Z}&\text{ if }k=1;\\
\mathbb{Z}&\text{ if }1<k<d.\end{cases}

Moreover,
\Phi^{[d]}(I(d))=0
.

Proof.

Recall that the Goodwillie-Burnside ring
A(d)=\pi_{0}\mathbbm{1}_{d}
is generated by the elements
[\lambda_{i}]
,
1\leq i\leq d
, see
[
ABH+25
, Def. 9.19 and Thm. 9.25]
. Moreover, by
[
ABH+25
, Lem. 9.22]
,
the homomorphism
\Phi^{[k]}\colon A(d)\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}
is given by

\Phi^{[k]}([\lambda_{i}])=|{\mathrm{Epi}}(k,i)|

and the augmentation ideal
I(d)=\mathrm{Ker}(\Phi^{[d]})\subset A(d)
is generated by the elements

[\lambda_{i}]-|{\mathrm{Epi}}(d,i)|\in I(d).

Therefore, we have

\Big(p,\Phi^{[k]}\big(I(d)\big)\Big)=\Big(p,|{\mathrm{Epi}}(k,1)|-|{\mathrm{Epi}}(d,1)|,\ldots,|{\mathrm{Epi}}(k,d)|-|{\mathrm{Epi}}(d,d)|\Big)\subset\mathbb{Z}.

The basic key observation is that
C_{p}
acts freely on the set
{\mathrm{Epi}}(p,i)
for
i\geq 2
and so
|{\mathrm{Epi}}(p,i)|
is a multiple of
p
in these cases. Hence, if
k=1
or
k=d
, then the assertion follows. Now let
1<k<d
. Again by the key observation, we see that

\Big(p,\Phi^{[k]}\big(I(d)\big)\Big)=\Big(p,|{\mathrm{Epi}}(k,2)|,\ldots,|{\mathrm{Epi}}(k,d)|\Big)\subset\mathbb{Z}.

Since
|{\mathrm{Epi}}(k,k)|=k!
is coprime to
p
, we obtain that
\Big(p,\Phi^{[k]}\big(I(d)\big)\Big)=\mathbb{Z}
.
∎

We are finally ready to give the proof of the Segal conjecture in the case
d=p
.

Proof of
5.4.3
.

That the map of interest is an equivalence may be checked by passing to the symmetric monoidal
[k]
–geometric fixed point functors
\mathrm{Exc}_{d}(\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega},\mathrm{Sp}^{\wedge}_{p})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}^{\wedge}_{p}
for all
1\leq k\leq d
. These are concretely given by
\Phi^{[k]}(-)\simeq\big(P_{\underline{1}}\mathrm{cr}_{k}(-)(\mathbb{S},\ldots,\mathbb{S})\big)^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq\big(\mathrm{cr}_{k}P_{k}(-)(\mathbb{S},\ldots,\mathbb{S})\big)^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq\big(\partial_{k}(-)\big)^{\wedge}_{p}
. To this end, recall Arone–Ching’s formula from
[
ABH+25
, Prop. 10.26]
which gives

(19)

\Big(\partial_{k}\big((((-)^{\otimes d})^{h\Sigma_{d}})^{\wedge}_{p}\big)\Big)^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq\Big(\partial_{k}\big(((-)^{\otimes d})^{h\Sigma_{d}}\big)\Big)^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq\prod_{\lambda\dashv d\colon|\lambda|=k}(\mathbb{S}^{t\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{nt}}(\lambda)})^{\wedge}_{p}.

Almost by construction of the Borelification functor
\beta_{*}\beta^{*}
, the map of interest is an equivalence on
\Phi^{[d]}
even before
p
–completion. On the other hand, by
5.4.8
and
Equation

19
, applying
\Phi^{[1]}
and invoking
5.4.11
and
5.4.9
gives

\mathbb{S}^{\wedge}_{p}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{S}^{t\mathcal{F}_{nt}(\Sigma_{p})}}^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq{\mathbb{S}^{tC_{p}}}^{\wedge}_{p},

which is an equivalence by the usual Segal conjecture in equivariant homotopy theory.
On the other hand, for
1<k<d
,
Equation

19
and
[
GL20
, Prop. A.8]
gives that
\partial_{k}\big(((-)^{\otimes d})^{h\Sigma_{d}}\big)^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq 0
; but then, also,
\big(p,\>\Phi^{[k]}(I(d))\big)=\mathbb{Z}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}
by
5.4.11
and so
\Phi^{[k]}((\mathbbm{1}_{d})^{\wedge}_{I(d),p})\simeq 0
. Hence, the map of interest becomes an equivalence upon applying
\Phi^{[k]}
for
1<k<d
also. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 5.4.12
.

From the computations in the preceding proof, one can easily check that the theorem would already fail when
d=p=3
had we not enforced the completion at the augmentation ideal. The case for
d=p=2
still works, of course, since this is the case of genuine
C_{2}
–spectra, as proved by Lin
[
Lin80
]
.
